Errors\n228\t        land in self._stdin_error rather than propagating out of start().\n229\t        \"\"\"\n230\t        if self._process is None or self._process.stdin is None:\n231\t            return\n232\t        fd = self._process.stdin.fileno()\n233\t        try:\n234\t            view = memoryview(payload)\n235\t            offset = 0\n236\t            while offset < len(view):\n237\t                chunk = view[offset : offset + self._STDIN_CHUNK_SIZE]\n238\t                while True:\n239\t                    try:\n240\t                        n = os.write(fd, chunk)\n241\t                        break\n242\t                    except InterruptedError:\n243\t                        # EINTR from signal delivery -- retry the same chunk.\n244\t                        continue\n245\t                if n <= 0:\n246\t                    # Defensive -- os.write should not return 0 on a pipe.\n247\t                    break\n248\t                offset += n\n249\t        except BrokenPipeError as exc:\n250\t            # Child exited before reading stdin; surface in wait()/terminate().\n251\t            self._stdin_error = exc\n252\t        except OSError as exc:\n253\t            self._stdin_error = exc\n254\t        finally:\n255\t            try:\n256\t                self._process.stdin.close()\n257\t            except Exception:  # pragma: no cover -- defensive\n258\t                pass\n259\t\n260\t    def wait(self) -> int:\n261\t        \"\"\"Wait for the process with timeout.            return 124  # match bash timeout exit code\n267\t\n268\t        rc = self._process.returncode if self._process.returncode is not None else -1\n269\t        if getattr(self, \"_stdin_error\", None) is not None:\n270\t            _log.warning(\n271\t                \"stdin_error pid=%s err=%r\", self._process.pid, self._stdin_error\n272\t            )\n273\t        if Component Inventory (what gets built)\n23\t\n24\t| # | Component | Type | Source path (SoT) | New / Reuse |\n25\t|---|-----------|------|-------------------|-------------|\n26\t| C1 | `sc:submit-pr` (working name) | Skill + command | `src/superclaude/skills/sc-submit-pr-protocol/` + `src/superclaude/commands/submit-pr.md` | **New** |\n27\t| C2 | Augment review poller | Skill ref/script | `…/sc-submit-pr-protocol/refs/augment-poll.md` (+ optional `scripts/poll-augment-review.sh`) | **New** |\n28\t| C3 | Severity→tier router | Skill ref | `…/refs/severity-routing.md` | **Reuse** rubric from `sc-auggie-review-protocol/refs/severity-rubric.md` |\n29\t| C4 | Reply-to-thread + resolve helper | Skill ref/script | `…/refs/thread-reply.md` | **New** (mirrors `gh api` patterns already in `sc-auggie-review-protocol`) |\n30\t| C5 | `offer-pr-review.sh` hook update | Hook | `src/superclaude/hooks/scripts/offer-pr-review.sh` | **Edit** — also mention `sc:submit-pr --monitor` |\n31\t| C6 | Tests | pytest + skill self-tests | `tests/…` | **New** |\n32\t\n33\tAfter any source edit: `make sync-dev` → `make verify-sync`. Never stage `.claude/` except\n34\t`settings.json`.\n35\t\n36\t## 2. Functional Requirements\n37\t\n38\t### FR-1 — PR submission skill with `--monitor` ordinal\n39\t`sc:submit-pr` opens a PR on the fork and arms the in-session monitor at the requested level.\n40\t\n41\t- `FR-1.1` Signature: `/sc:submit-pr [--monitor {0,1,2,3}] [--max-rounds N] [--base master] [--head <branch>] [--title …] [--body …]`\n42\t- `FR-1.2` `--monitor` defaults to `0` (open PR, no monitor — behaviorally identical to today).\n43\t- `FR-1.3` All `gh pr create` / review / reply calls **MUST** pin `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`.\n44\t- `FR-1.4` Pre-PR checks from `CLAUDE.md` are enforced (confirm `origin`, rebase onto\n45\t  `origin/master` if behind, verify returned URL is `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`).\n46\t- `FR-1.5` On `--monitor ≥ 1`, after the PR is created the skill arms the Monitor tool (§FR-2).\n47\t\n48\t### FR-2 — In-session Augment review monitor\n49\t- `FR-2.1` Poll the PR for the **Augment Code GitHub App** review using `gh` (e.g.\n50\t  `gh pr view <N> --repo … --json reviews,comments` and\n51\t  `gh api repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/pulls/<N>/comments`).\n52\t- `FR-2.2` Detection contract: distinguish three states — (a) *no review yet* (keep polling),\n53\t  (b) *review posted, zero Medium+ findings* (terminate clean), (c) *review posted with\n54\t  findings* (route). Detection keys on the Augment App's bot login (resolved empirically per\n55\t  OQ1, stored as a config constant, not hard-guessed).\n56\t- `FR-2.3` Poll interval ≥ 30s (GitHub API rate-limit friendly); overall wait bounded by a\n57\t  timeout (default ~30 min, configurable) after which the monitor reports \"review never\n58\t  arrived\" and exits gracefully.\n59\t- `FR-2.4` The monitor is hosted by the **Monitor tool**; the live session must remain open.\n60\t  On session close before review lands, the monitor is lost (documented limitation; V2.0 fixes\n61\t  via headless host).\n62\t\n63\t### FR-3 — Severity → troubleshoot-tier routing\n64\t- `FR-3.1` For each finding, derive a normalized severity (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Nit) by\n65\t  re-grading through the reused **severity rubric** (Augment's self-reported severity is a\n66\t  *hint*, not authoritative — same principle as `sc-auggie-review`).\n67\t- `FR-3.2` Route: **Medium → `/sc:troubleshoot --fix`**; **High or Critical →\n68\t  `/sc:troubleshoot --depth deep --fix`**; **Low / Nit → report only**, no remediation.\n69\t- `FR-3.3` The troubleshoot invocation is seeded with the finding body + `file:line` +\n70\t  evidence so the diagnosis is grounded (no re-deriving the problem from scratch).\n71\t- `FR-3.4` Multiple findings batch sensibly: group by file/area where troubleshoot can address\n72\t  several in one session; never exceed the round budget.\n73\t\n74\t### FR-4 — Autonomy gates (the `--monitor` ordinal)\n75\t- `FR-4.1` **Level 1**: diagnose + propose only. Surface to the user: all Augment comments,\n76\t  the proposed remediation for each, and an explicit offer (\"fix these? y/n\"). **No edits.**\n77\t- `FR-4.2` **Level 2**: implement fixes + validate locally (§FR-5). Then **HALT**: ask the\n78\t  user before any `git commit`/`git push` and before posting any reply. Working tree changes\n79\t  are left in place for inspection.\n80\t- `FR-4.3` **Level 3**: implement + validate + commit + push to the PR branch + reply-to-thread\n81\t  + resolve, unattended — governed by the loop-guard (§FR-6).\n82\t- `FR-4.4` `needs_human_decision`-class findings (ambiguous intent, security trade-offs, API\n83\t  contract changes) **MUST HALT for human sign-off even at level 3** — never auto-ship a\n84\t  guessed default. (Mirrors the repo's \"human-decision items must HALT\" rule.)\n85\t\n86\t### FR-5 — Local validation before push\n87\t- `FR-5.1` \"Validated\" = the relevant test command passes. Default: targeted tests for changed\n88\t  files; escalate to `make test` when changes are cross-cutting (decision recorded per round).\n89\t- `FR-5.2` `make lint` **and** `uv run ruff format --check src/ tests/` must pass before push\n90\t  (green `make lint` ≠ green CI format — known repo gotcha).\n91\t- `FR-5.3` If validation fails, do **not** push; report the failure and (level 3) retry within\n92\t  the round budget or HALT.\n93\t\n94\t### FR-6 — Reply, resolve, and loop termination\n95\t- `FR-6.1` Each implemented fix posts a reply on the **specific** Augment review-comment thread\n96\t  (`gh api …/pulls/<N>/comments/<comment_id>/replies` or the review-comment reply endpoint),\n97\t  summarizing the fix + commit SHA, then resolves the thread.\n98\t- `FR-6.2` **Loop-stop**: after a remediation round + push, wait for Augment's re-review; stop\n99\t  when the re-review surfaces **zero Medium+ findings** OR `--max-rounds` is reached.\n100\t- `FR-6.3` **Loop-guard**: a re-review caused by the monitor's own push counts as the *next*\n101\t  round, not a new independent trigger; the round counter is monotonic and capped. Never exceed\n102\t  `--max-rounds` (default 2, max 5).\n103\t- `FR-6.4` On max-rounds-with-residual-findings, post a summary comment listing unresolved\n104\t  findings and hand back to the human.\n105\t\n106\t### FR-7 — Hook integration (minimal)\n107\t- `FR-7.1` Update `offer-pr-review.sh` to additionally mention `sc:submit-pr --monitor` as the\n108\t  autonomous path (alongside the existing `/sc:auggie-review` offer). Non-Functional Requirements\n112\t- `NFR-1` Idempotent replies: never double-post the same fix reply to a thread (track replied\n113\t  comment IDs).\n114\t- `NFR-2` Rate-limit safety: poll ≥30s; back off on `gh` API 403/secondary-limit.\n115\t- `NFR-3` Observability: write a per-run log (`.dev/…/monitor-run-<PR>.jsonl`) of every poll,\n116\t  finding, route decision, fix, push, reply — for forensic review and round auditing.\n117\t- `NFR-4` Fail-safe defaults: unknown severity → treat as Medium (fix tier), not Critical or\n118\t  ignore; unknown bot login → \"review not detected\" (never act on a non-Augment comment).\n119\t- `NFR-5` All paths absolute in user-facing prompts; all paste-ready commands single-line.\n120\t\n121\t## 4. Red-Team — the four risks that can sink this\n151\t\n152\t> These are the points where a naive build breaks. Never stage `.claude/` except\n`settings.json`.\n\n## 2. Functional Requirements\n\n### FR-1 — PR submission skill with `--monitor` ordinal\n`sc:submit-pr` opens a PR on the fork and arms the in-session monitor at the requested level.\n\n- `FR-1.1` Signature: `/sc:submit-pr [--monitor {0,1,2,3}] [--max-rounds N] [--base master] [--head <branch>] [--title …] [--body …]`\n- `FR-1.2` `--monitor` defaults to `0` (open PR, no monitor — behaviorally identical to today).\n- `FR-1.3` All `gh pr create` / review / reply calls **MUST** pin `--repo IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`.\n- `FR-1.4` Pre-PR checks from `CLAUDE.md` are enforced (confirm `origin`, rebase onto\n  `origin/master` if behind, verify returned URL is `IronbellyOrg/IronClaude`).\n- `FR-1.5` On `--monitor ≥ 1`, after the PR is created the skill arms the Monitor tool (§FR-2).\n\n### FR-2 — In-session Augment review monitor\n- `FR-2.1` Poll the PR for the **Augment Code GitHub App** review using `gh` (e.g.\n  `gh pr view <N> --repo … --json reviews,comments` and\n  `gh api repos/IronbellyOrg/IronClaude/pulls/<N>/comments`).\n- `FR-2.2` Detection contract: distinguish three states — (a) *no review yet* (keep polling),\n  (b) *review posted, zero Medium+ findings* (terminate clean), (c) *review posted with\n  findings* (route). Detection keys on the Augment App's bot login (resolved empirically per\n  OQ1, stored as a config constant, not hard-guessed).\n- `FR-2.3` Poll interval ≥ 30s (GitHub API rate-limit friendly); overall wait bounded by a\n  timeout (default ~30 min, configurable) after which the monitor reports \"review never\n  arrived\" and exits gracefully.\n- `FR-2.4` The monitor is hosted by the **Monitor tool**; the live session must remain open.\n  On session close before review lands, the monitor is lost (documented limitation; V2.0 fixes\n  via headless host).\n\n### FR-3 — Severity → troubleshoot-tier routing\n- `FR-3.1` For each finding, derive a normalized severity (Critical/High/Medium/Low/Nit) by\n  re-grading through the reused **severity rubric** (Augment's self-reported severity is a\n  *hint*, not authoritative — same principle as `sc-auggie-review`).\n- `FR-3.2` Route: **Medium → `/sc:troubleshoot --fix`**; **High or Critical →\n  `/sc:troubleshoot --depth deep --fix`**; **Low / Nit → report only**, no remediation.\n- `FR-3.3` The troubleshoot invocation is seeded with the finding body + `file:line` +\n  evidence so the diagnosis is grounded (no re-deriving the problem from scratch).\n- `FR-3.4` Multiple findings batch sensibly: group by file/area where troubleshoot can address\n  several in one session; never exceed the round budget.\n\n### FR-4 — Autonomy gates (the `--monitor` ordinal)\n- `FR-4.1` **Level 1**: diagnose + propose only. Surface to the user: all Augment comments,\n  the proposed remediation for each, and an explicit offer (\"fix these? y/n\"). **No edits.**\n- `FR-4.2` **Level 2**: implement fixes + validate locally (§FR-5). Then **HALT**: ask the\n  user before any `git commit`/`git push` and before posting any reply. Working tree changes\n  are left in place for inspection.\n- `FR-4.3` **Level 3**: implement + validate + commit + push to the PR branch + reply-to-thread\n  + resolve, unattended — governed by the loop-guard (§FR-6).\n- `FR-4.4` `needs_human_decision`-class findings (ambiguous intent, security trade-offs, API\n  contract changes) **MUST HALT for human sign-off even at level 3** — never auto-ship a\n  guessed default. (Mirrors the repo's \"human-decision items must HALT\" rule.)\n\n### FR-5 — Local validation before push\n- `FR-5.1` \"Validated\" = the relevant test command passes. Default: targeted tests for changed\n  files; escalate to `make test` when changes are cross-cutting (decision recorded per round).\n- `FR-5.2` `make lint` **and** `uv run ruff format --check src/ tests/` must pass before push\n  (green `make lint` ≠ green CI format — known repo gotcha).\n- `FR-5.3` If validation fails, do **not** push; report the failure and (level 3) retry within\n  the round budget or HALT.\n\n### FR-6 — Reply, resolve, and loop termination\n- `FR-6.1` Each implemented fix posts a reply on the **specific** Augment review-comment thread\n  (`gh api …/pulls/<N>/comments/<comment_id>/replies` or the review-comment reply endpoint),\n  summarizing the fix + commit SHA, then resolves the thread.\n- `FR-6.2` **Loop-stop**: after a remediation round + push, wait for Augment's re-review; stop\n  when the re-review surfaces **zero Medium+ findings** OR `--max-rounds` is reached.\n- `FR-6.3` **Loop-guard**: a re-review caused by the monitor's own push counts as the *next*\n  round, not a new independent trigger; the round counter is monotonic and capped. Never exceed\n  `--max-rounds` (default 2, max 5).\n- `FR-6.4` On max-rounds-with-residual-findings, post a summary comment listing unresolved\n  findings and hand back to the human.\n\n### FR-7 — Hook integration (minimal)\n- `FR-7.1` Update `offer-pr-review.sh` to additionally mention `sc:submit-pr --monitor` as the\n  autonomous path (alongside the existing `/sc:auggie-review` offer). Hook stays **fail-open**,\n  never blocks, never spawns anything.\n\n## 3. Non-Functional Requirements\n- `NFR-1` Idempotent replies: never double-post the same fix reply to a thread (track replied\n  comment IDs).\n- `NFR-2` Rate-limit safety: poll ≥30s; back off on `gh` API 403/secondary-limit.\n- `NFR-3` Observability: write a per-run log (`.dev/…/monitor-run-<PR>.jsonl`) of every poll,\n  finding, route decision, fix, push, reply — for forensic review and round auditing.\n- `NFR-4` Fail-safe defaults: unknown severity → treat as Medium (fix tier), not Critical or\n  ignore; unknown bot login → \"review not detected\" (never act on a non-Augment comment).\n- `NFR-5` All paths absolute in user-facing prompts; all paste-ready commands single-line.\n\n## 4. Red-Team — the four risks that can sink this\n\n> These are the points where a naive build breaks. Treated as MUST-resolve-before-build.\n\n- **R1 (OQ1) — Augment review-detection is guesswork until probed.** We do **not** know the\n  Augment App's exact GitHub emission shape (formal review vs. issue comments vs. check-run) or\n  its bot login. **Mitigation:** before writing the parser, run an empirical probe — open one\n  throwaway PR, let Augment review it, capture `gh pr view --json reviews,comments` +\n  `gh api …/pulls/<N>/reviews` + `…/comments`, and lock the detection constant from real data.\n  Building the parser on a guess is the #1 failure mode.\n- **R2 — Loop-guard correctness (infinite remediation).** The monitor pushes a fix → Augment\n  re-reviews → possibly new findings → fix → … A monotonic, capped round counter (FR-6.3) is\n  **load-bearing**; an off-by-one or a \"new review = new trigger\" bug burns compute and spams\n  the PR. **Mitigation:** AC-6 fixture + the round counter must key on \"reviews observed since\n  arm\", not \"reviews seen since last poll\".\n- **R3 — Session-longevity fragility (in-session host).** Augment's review can take minutes;\n  the Monitor tool requires the session to stay open. Spec citation is **[CODE-VERIFIED]**.\n- **Prompt via stdin, not argv (162–217, 221–258):** `start()` writes the prompt to child stdin in 64 KiB chunks with EINTR-retry and BrokenPipe capture, closing stdin in `finally` to deliver EOF. This is the \"bypasses 128KB argv limit\" property the spec relies on for large prompt envelopes (§7). **[CODE-VERIFIED]** — the comment at lines 124–125 and 194–197 confirms the MAX_ARG_STRLEN rationale.\n- **Pre-spawn size guard:** `PromptTooLargeForArgv` raised when encoded prompt > `PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` (default 16 MiB, env-overridable via `SUPERCLAUDE_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`, lines 56–58, 169–173).\n- **Lifecycle hooks:** `on_spawn(pid)`, `on_signal(pid, sig)`, `on_exit(pid, rc)` — all optional. Useful for the spec's audit-log events (`claude_process_spawn`, §14) without subclassing.\n- **`timeout_seconds` default 6300** (~105 min); `wait()` returns `124` on timeout (bash-compatible). The spec's `StuckRun` alert (§14) keys on `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds` — that attribute is real and instance-accessible (line 109). **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n- **`tool_write_mode`** (101, 176–178, 325–345): when True, the LLM writes the output file via the Write tool and stdout is redirected to `.log`; `validate_tool_write_output()` confirms the file exists + is non-empty. A caller passing `env_vars={...}` cannot *remove* inherited secrets — `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `~/.aienv`-sourced vars, etc. all flow into the child via `os.environ.copy()`. The spec's §7 / INV-001 / SC-7 requirement (\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` … No `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\") is therefore a **real, code-grounded change**, not a hypothetical. AC-7 (Runner `/proc/<pid>/environ` contains no token) **cannot pass against the primitive as-is** — the Runner wrapper (R2) must build a minimal env dict from scratch and the reuse must either (a) add an allowlist/replace-mode parameter to `build_env()`, or (b) have the wrapper bypass `build_env()` entirely. This is the single highest-leverage implementation finding for the V2 build.\n\n### Reuse-fit assessment\n- **Fits as-is:** stdin delivery, process-group kill, timeout→124, max_turns, stream-json, lifecycle hooks. The Runner can instantiate it inside the sandbox with `cwd=<checkout>` (Popen inherits cwd from the process; note `start()` does **not** set `cwd` explicitly — it relies on the spawning process's cwd, so the Runner must `os.chdir` or the primitive needs a `cwd` param. **Gap:** there is no `cwd` kwarg on `ClaudeProcess` — spec §7 says \"`cwd` = sandbox checkout\" but the primitive has no such parameter. **[CODE-CONTRADICTED]** — spec assumes a `cwd` knob that does not exist; either add one or chdir in the Runner entrypoint.)\n- **Needs change:** env isolation (above); optional `cwd` parameter.\n\n**Key Takeaways**\n- `ClaudeProcess` at `process.py:72` is real and matches the spec's command/stdin claims exactly.\n- `build_env()` is additive-only (`os.environ.copy()`); the allowlist-env requirement (INV-001/SC-7/AC-7) is code-verified-necessary, not speculative.\n- No `cwd` parameter exists — spec §7's \"cwd = sandbox checkout\" needs either a new kwarg or a chdir in the Runner; flag for TDD.\n- Lifecycle hooks + `timeout_seconds` cleanly support the spec's audit events and `StuckRun` alert.\n\n---\n\n## V1.0 spec (`../20260610-234750-pr-review-auto-remediation/merged-requirements.md`)\n\nThe direct predecessor. default edge\n231\t        return MonitorState.S4_PUSHING\n232\t    if edge == (MonitorState.S7_VALIDATING, \"validation_failed\"):\n233\t        return MonitorState.VALIDATION_FAIL\n234\t    if edge == (MonitorState.S4_PUSHING, \"pushed\"):\n235\t        return MonitorState.S6_REPLYING\n236\t    if edge == (MonitorState.S6_REPLYING, \"replied\"):\n237\t        return MonitorState.RESOLVING\n238\t    if edge == (MonitorState.RESOLVING, \"resolved\"):\n239\t        return MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW\n240\t    if edge == (MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW, \"rereview_attributed\"):\n241\t        return MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY  # loop-guard increments at this edge (INV-001)\n242\t    if edge == (MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW, \"timeout\"):\n243\t        return MonitorState.TERMINAL_TIMEOUT\n244\t\n245\t    # Unknown edge: stay put (defensive; a finding explicitly ``\"unverified\"`` is\n262\t    a false positive (FR-3.5) and is NOT verified. Side effects via seams.\n399\t        config.do_push(pre_push_sha=None)\n400\t        result.push_count += 1\n401\t        config.do_reply(applied_edits=result.applied_edits)\n402\t        result.reply_count += 1\n403\t        config.do_resolve()\n404\t        result.state = MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW\n405\t\n406\t        # Re-review attributed to our push: tick the monotonic round counter (INV-001).\n407\t        result.round_counter += 1\n408\t\n409\t        # If there is a next residual cycle, loop; else clean re-review terminates.\n410\t        if cycle_index + 1 >= len(cycles):\n411\t            result.state = MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n412\t            break\n413\t\n414\t    return result\n415\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"6806b18f-f772-4ce0-b018-e83eb7cbe57e","uuid":"c684e15b-5eb6-4342-843c-f075ffc1a93c","timestamp":"2026-06-11T12:26:35.850Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/pr_submit/fsm.py","content":"\"\"\"The single finite state machine for ``sc:pr-submit`` (spec §5).\n\nEncodes the gate table as three one-line ordinal checks (G-arm / G-edit / G-push)\nplus the ``needs_human_decision`` pre-gate override (§5.2), the INV-016 5-predicate\nG-push conjunction (§5.3), and the ``run_skill(...)`` integration driver that the\nspec's test bodies call and assert on (returns a :class:`~superclaude.pr_submit.models.SkillResult`).\n\nNFR-6 / AC-9 / FR-G1 core purity: this module imports NO ``anthropic`` SDK and\ncontains ZERO shell / version-control command tokens. into :class:`SkillArgs`, enforcing the documented bounds.\n\n    Raises :class:`ValueError` with the EXACT spec message when ``--poll-interval``\n    is below 30 (T-111: \"minimum is 30 seconds\") or ``--max-rounds`` exceeds the\n    hard cap of 5 (T-102). ``--monitor`` choices are enforced by argparse (T-101).\n    \"\"\"\n    parser = build_arg_parser()\n    ns = parser.parse_args(argv)\n    if ns.poll_interval < MIN_POLL_INTERVAL:\n        raise ValueError(f\"--poll-interval {POLL_INTERVAL_ERROR}\")\n    if ns.max_rounds > HARD_CAP_MAX_ROUNDS:\n        raise ValueError(MAX_ROUNDS_ERROR)\n    if ns.max_rounds < 1:\n        raise ValueError(\"--max-rounds must be >= 1\")\n    return SkillArgs(\n        monitor=ns.monitor,\n        max_rounds=ns.max_rounds,\n        poll_interval=ns.poll_interval,\n        timeout=ns.timeout,\n        base=ns.base,\n        head=ns.head,\n        title=ns.title,\n        body=ns.body,\n        output_dir=ns.output_dir,\n        resume=ns.resume,\n        yes=ns.yes,\n    )\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# Gate predicates (spec §5.2 / §5.3) — one-line ordinal checks, never nested ifs\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef gate_arm(ordinal: int) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"G-arm: ``ordinal >= 1`` to leave S0_IDLE and enter polling.\"\"\"\n    return ordinal >= 1\n\n\ndef gate_edit(ordinal: int) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"G-edit: ``ordinal >= 2`` to enter S3_FIXING; else route to PROPOSED.\"\"\"\n    return ordinal >= 2\n\n\ndef should_halt_rounds(round_counter: int, max_rounds: int) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Round-budget gate: ``round_counter >= max_rounds`` (INV-5, ``>=`` not ``>``).\n\n    Mirrors the canonical loop-guard predicate (Phase 8 ``loop_guard.py`` owns the\n    standalone-tested copy; this in-FSM check uses identical ``>=`` semantics).\n    \"\"\"\n    return round_counter >= max_rounds\n\n\ndef evaluate_push_decision(\n    *,\n    monitor_ordinal: int,\n    validation_status: str,\n    needs_human_decision: bool,\n    round_counter: int,\n    max_rounds: int,\n    applied_edits: int,\n) -> PushDecision:\n    \"\"\"Evaluate the INV-016 5-predicate G-push conjunction (spec §5.3, verbatim).\n\n    A push is authorized iff ALL five predicates hold, ANDed immediately before the\n    push: (1) ``monitor_ordinal >= 3``; (2) ``validation_status == \"validated\"``;\n    (3) ``needs_human_decision == false`` for every finding; (4)\n    ``round_counter < max_rounds``; (5) ``applied_edits > 0``. The returned\n    :class:`PushDecision` records each predicate independently (the write-ahead\n    audit primitive) so callers can assert the exact gate that blocked.\n    \"\"\"\n    p1 = monitor_ordinal >= 3\n    p2 = validation_status == \"validated\"\n    p3 = not needs_human_decision\n    p4 = round_counter < max_rounds\n    p5 = applied_edits  # int; truthy when > 0\n    authorized = bool(p1 and p2 and p3 and p4 and p5 > 0)\n    return PushDecision(\n        predicate_1_ordinal=p1,\n        predicate_2_validated=p2,\n        predicate_3_no_human=p3,\n        predicate_4_under_cap=p4,\n        predicate_5_applied_edits=p5,\n        authorized=authorized,\n    )\n\n\ndef push_fail_state(decision: PushDecision) -> MonitorState:\n    \"\"\"Map a blocked :class:`PushDecision` to its fail-route terminal (§5.3).\n\n    predicate 3 → HALT_HUMAN; 4 → HALT_MAX_ROUNDS; 5 → TERMINAL_CLEAN (report);\n    1–2 → REPORT_ONLY. Evaluated in spec order so the most-specific HALT wins.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not decision.predicate_3_no_human:\n        return MonitorState.HALT_HUMAN\n    if not decision.predicate_4_under_cap:\n        return MonitorState.HALT_MAX_ROUNDS\n    if decision.predicate_5_applied_edits <= 0:\n        return MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n    return MonitorState.REPORT_ONLY\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# transition() — the transition table (spec §5.4: a table, not nested ifs)\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef transition(state: MonitorState, event: str, context: dict | None = None) -> MonitorState:\n    \"\"\"Return the next state for ``(state, event)`` under the ordinal ceiling.\n\n    The ``needs_human_decision`` override is evaluated FIRST (pre-gate, §5.2): any\n    event in a cycle carrying ``needs_human_decision`` routes to HALT_HUMAN\n    regardless of ordinal. Otherwise the edge is looked up in the transition table,\n    with the three ordinal gates applied at their edges.\n    \"\"\"\n    ctx = context or {}\n    ordinal = int(ctx.get(\"monitor_ordinal\", 0))\n\n    # Pre-gate override (the ONLY ceiling short-circuit).\n    if ctx.get(\"needs_human_decision\"):\n        return MonitorState.HALT_HUMAN\n\n    edge = (state, event)\n\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S0_IDLE, \"arm\"):\n        return MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY if gate_arm(ordinal) else MonitorState.S0_IDLE\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY, \"no_review\"):\n        return MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY  # loop back (poll again)\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY, \"clean\"):\n        return MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY, \"findings\"):\n        # round-budget gate evaluated here\n        if should_halt_rounds(int(ctx.get(\"round_counter\", 0)), int(ctx.get(\"max_rounds\", DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS))):\n            return MonitorState.HALT_MAX_ROUNDS\n        return MonitorState.S2B_VERIFY\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S2B_VERIFY, \"verified\"):\n        return MonitorState.S3_DIAGNOSE\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S2B_VERIFY, \"unverified\"):\n        return MonitorState.REPORT_ONLY  # no round consumed\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S3_DIAGNOSE, \"diagnosed\"):\n        # G-edit: ordinal >= 2 enters S3_FIXING; else PROPOSED (offer, no edits)\n        return MonitorState.S3_FIXING if gate_edit(ordinal) else MonitorState.PROPOSED\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S3_FIXING, \"edits_applied\"):\n        return MonitorState.S7_VALIDATING\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S7_VALIDATING, \"validated\"):\n        # G-push handled by evaluate_push_decision at the caller; default edge\n        return MonitorState.S4_PUSHING\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S7_VALIDATING, \"validation_failed\"):\n        return MonitorState.VALIDATION_FAIL\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S4_PUSHING, \"pushed\"):\n        return MonitorState.S6_REPLYING\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S6_REPLYING, \"replied\"):\n        return MonitorState.RESOLVING\n    if edge == (MonitorState.RESOLVING, \"resolved\"):\n        return MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW, \"rereview_attributed\"):\n        return MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY  # loop-guard increments at this edge (INV-001)\n    if edge == (MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW, \"timeout\"):\n        return MonitorState.TERMINAL_TIMEOUT\n\n    # Unknown edge: stay put (defensive; real runs never hit this).\n    return state\n\n\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n# run_skill() — the integration driver the spec test bodies call\n# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n\ndef _noop(*_args, **_kwargs) -> None:\n    return None\n\n\ndef _default_verify(finding: Finding) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Default verify-before-remediate: a finding is verified unless flagged unverified.\n\n    Fixtures carry ``verification_status``; a finding explicitly ``\"unverified\"`` is\n    a false positive (FR-3.5) and is NOT verified. Otherwise it is treated as\n    grounded (the real grounding spawns the evidence-validator agent — injected via\n    the ``verify`` seam in production).\n    \"\"\"\n    return finding.verification_status != \"unverified\"\n\n\ndef _default_apply_edits(findings: list[Finding]) -> int:\n    \"\"\"Default edit application: count groundable, verified, in-diff findings.\n\n    An ungroundable finding (``in_diff is False`` or unverified) yields no applied\n    edit — the INV-016 predicate-5 ``applied_edits == 0`` path (T-ZERO-EDIT-NO-PUSH).\n    \"\"\"\n    return sum(1 for f in findings if f.in_diff and f.verification_status != \"unverified\")\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass RunConfig:\n    \"\"\"Injected seams + inputs for :func:`run_skill` (keeps the core pure/testable).\"\"\"\n\n    monitor_ordinal: int = 0\n    max_rounds: int = DEFAULT_MAX_ROUNDS\n    poll_interval: int = MIN_POLL_INTERVAL\n    timeout: int = DEFAULT_TIMEOUT\n    pr_number: int | None = None\n    resume: str | None = None\n    # The findings for the (first) cycle, already classified/normalized.\n    findings: list[Finding] = field(default_factory=list)\n    # Optional multi-round sequence: each entry is the residual finding set observed\n    # at the next re-review (drives the loop-guard off-by-one tests, Phase 8).\n    rereview_findings: list[list[Finding]] = field(default_factory=list)\n    review_state: str = \"findings\"  # \"polling\" | \"clean\" | \"findings\"\n    # Side-effect seams (defaults keep the core pure / recording-only).\n    arm_monitor: Callable[..., None] = _noop\n    verify: Callable[[Finding], bool] = _default_verify\n    apply_edits: Callable[[list[Finding]], int] = _default_apply_edits\n    run_validation: Callable[..., str] = staticmethod(lambda **_: \"validated\")\n    do_push: Callable[..., None] = _noop\n    do_reply: Callable[..., None] = _noop\n    do_resolve: Callable[..., None] = _noop\n\n\ndef run_skill(config: RunConfig | None = None, **overrides) -> SkillResult:\n    \"\"\"Drive the FSM over the configured inputs and return a :class:`SkillResult`.\n\n    Honors the capability ceiling: at L0 the FSM never leaves S0_IDLE (AC-1); at L1\n    it verifies + proposes but applies NO edits; at L2 it applies edits + validates\n    then HALTs before any push/reply (``S4'_HALT_BEFORE_PUSH``); at L3 it pushes\n    (only when the INV-016 conjunction holds), replies, resolves, and loops under\n    the monotonic round counter. The ``needs_human_decision`` override HALTs at any\n    armed ordinal.\n    \"\"\"\n    if config is None:\n        config = RunConfig(**overrides)\n    elif overrides:\n        for k, v in overrides.items():\n            setattr(config, k, v)\n\n    result = SkillResult(state=MonitorState.S0_IDLE)\n    ordinal = config.monitor_ordinal\n\n    # G-arm: L0 returns immediately, byte-identical to today (AC-1).\n    if not gate_arm(ordinal):\n        result.state = MonitorState.S0_IDLE\n        return result\n    config.arm_monitor(config.pr_number)\n    result.state = MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY\n\n    if config.review_state == \"polling\":\n        result.state = MonitorState.S2_CLASSIFY\n        return result\n    if config.review_state == \"clean\":\n        result.state = MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n        return result\n\n    # Multi-round loop: cycle 0 is `findings`; subsequent residuals come from\n    # `rereview_findings`. The round counter ticks on each attributed re-review\n    # (INV-001) — modeled here as advancing to the next residual set.\n    cycles = [config.findings] + list(config.rereview_findings)\n    for cycle_index, cycle_findings in enumerate(cycles):\n        # Round-budget gate (evaluated at S2_CLASSIFY, before opening a fix cycle).\n        if should_halt_rounds(result.round_counter, config.max_rounds):\n            result.state = MonitorState.HALT_MAX_ROUNDS\n            break\n\n        result.findings = cycle_findings\n        if not cycle_findings:\n            result.state = MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n            break\n\n        # Pre-gate override: any needs_human_decision finding HALTs (FR-4.4).\n        if any(f.needs_human_decision for f in cycle_findings):\n            result.state = MonitorState.HALT_HUMAN\n            break\n\n        # S2b_VERIFY (FR-3.5): unverified findings route to REPORT_ONLY, no round.\n        verified = [f for f in cycle_findings if config.verify(f)]\n        if not verified:\n            result.state = MonitorState.REPORT_ONLY\n            break\n\n        # S3_DIAGNOSE → G-edit.\n        if not gate_edit(ordinal):\n            # L1 ceiling: propose, apply NO edits. Emit the exact \"fix these? y/n\".\n            result.state = MonitorState.PROPOSED\n            result.applied_edits = 0\n            result.proposal = PROPOSE_PROMPT\n            break\n\n        # S3_FIXING: apply the diagnosed edits in the working tree.\n        result.applied_edits = config.apply_edits(verified)\n\n        # S7_VALIDATING.\n        result.validation_status = config.run_validation(findings=verified)\n        if result.validation_status != \"validated\":\n            result.state = MonitorState.VALIDATION_FAIL\n            break\n\n        # G-push (INV-016 conjunction).\n        decision = evaluate_push_decision(\n            monitor_ordinal=ordinal,\n            validation_status=result.validation_status,\n            needs_human_decision=any(f.needs_human_decision for f in verified),\n            round_counter=result.round_counter,\n            max_rounds=config.max_rounds,\n            applied_edits=result.applied_edits,\n        )\n        result.push_decision = decision\n        if not decision.authorized:\n            # L2 ceiling (ordinal < 3) halts before push; other blocks route per §5.3.\n            if ordinal < 3:\n                result.state = MonitorState.S4_HALT_BEFORE_PUSH\n            else:\n                result.state = push_fail_state(decision)\n            break\n\n        # S4_PUSHING → S6_REPLYING → RESOLVING (L3). Side effects via seams.\n        config.do_push(pre_push_sha=None)\n        result.push_count += 1\n        config.do_reply(applied_edits=result.applied_edits)\n        result.reply_count += 1\n        config.do_resolve()\n        result.state = MonitorState.S5_AWAITING_REREVIEW\n\n        # Re-review attributed to our push: tick the monotonic round counter (INV-001).\n        result.round_counter += 1\n\n        # If there is a next residual cycle, loop; else clean re-review terminates.\n        if cycle_index + 1 >= len(cycles):\n            result.state = MonitorState.TERMINAL_CLEAN\n            break\n\n    return result\n","numLines":415,"startLine":1,"totalLines":415}}}

All `gh`/`git` I/O lives in the **skill's bash scripts**; the Python core only *records decisions* via injected recording-only callable seams (`fsm.RunConfig.do_push/do_reply/do_resolve = _noop`). **V2 inverts this layering:** V2 is a `superclaude remediate` **CLI group that performs the gh/git itself** (H3 push, H4 reply/resolve, H5 gh-wrapper). Reuse the pure brain, supply real hands.\n- V1's idempotency `fix_key` (content hash) ≠ V2's claim key (`trigger_comment_id, flag_hash`) — both belong in V2 for different jobs (cross-round dedup vs. trigger claim mutex).\n\n---\n\n## Swarm bounded-counter citation (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`) — IMPRECISE\n\nThe V2 spec §9 says the round/budget counter \"mirrors swarm bounded-counter (`cli/swarm/commands.py:2269`): monotonic, disk-authoritative, survives restarts.\" **Line 2269 is actually the in-memory `--watch` iteration cap** (`iterations += 1` inside `status_cmd`'s watch loop, bounded by `watch_max_iterations`; `commands.py:2268-2270`) — it is NOT disk-authoritative and does NOT survive restarts. And the closest *monotonic round counter* idiom in the repo is actually **`pr_submit.SkillResult.round_counter`** (above), not swarm at all.\n\n**Implication for V2:** the swarm:2269 citation is a stale/imprecise line anchor. Read in full.\n\n### What it does (the verified primitive)\n- **`class ClaudeProcess`** is defined at **line 72** [CODE-VERIFIED] — the spec's `cli/pipeline/process.py:72` citation is **accurate**.\n- Manages one `claude --print` subprocess with process-group signal handling (`os.setpgrp` → `os.killpg`) so the whole child tree dies on shutdown (`terminate()`, lines 278–323).\n- **`build_command()` (121–143)** emits exactly what the spec §7 claims: `claude --print --verbose --dangerously-skip-permissions --no-session-persistence --tools default --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` then `--model` (if set) then `extra_args`. Spec citation is **[CODE-VERIFIED]**.\n- **Prompt via stdin, not argv (162–217, 221–258):** `start()` writes the prompt to child stdin in 64 KiB chunks with EINTR-retry and BrokenPipe capture, closing stdin in `finally` to deliver EOF. This is the \"bypasses 128KB argv limit\" property the spec relies on for large prompt envelopes (§7). **[CODE-VERIFIED]** — the comment at lines 124–125 and 194–197 confirms the MAX_ARG_STRLEN rationale.\n- **Pre-spawn size guard:** `PromptTooLargeForArgv` raised when encoded prompt > `PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` (default 16 MiB, env-overridable via `SUPERCLAUDE_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`, lines 56–58, 169–173).\n- **Lifecycle hooks:** `on_spawn(pid)`, `on_signal(pid, sig)`, `on_exit(pid, rc)` — all optional. Useful for the spec's audit-log events (`claude_process_spawn`, §14) without subclassing.\n- **`timeout_seconds` default 6300** (~105 min); `wait()` returns `124` on timeout (bash-compatible). The spec's `StuckRun` alert (§14) keys on `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds` — that attribute is real and instance-accessible (line 109). **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n- **`tool_write_mode`** (101, 176–178, 325–345): when True, the LLM writes the output file via the Write tool and stdout is redirected to `.log`; `validate_tool_write_output()` confirms the file exists + is non-empty. Relevant if the Runner emits its diff via a tool-written artifact rather than stdout.\n- **`output_format`** default `\"stream-json\"` — matches spec §7's `stream-json` progress-capture requirement.\n\n### ⚠️ Critical gap the spec correctly flags — `build_env()` is NOT an allowlist [CODE-VERIFIED]\n`build_env()` (lines 145–160) does:\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full host environment\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # callers can only ADD/override, never restrict\nreturn env\n```\nThis is **additive-only**. A caller passing `env_vars={...}` cannot *remove* inherited secrets — `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `~/.aienv`-sourced vars, etc. all flow into the child via `os.environ.copy()`. The spec's §7 / INV-001 / SC-7 requirement (\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` … No `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\") is therefore a **real, code-grounded change**, not a hypothetical. AC-7 (Runner `/proc/<pid>/environ` contains no token) **cannot pass against the primitive as-is** — the Runner wrapper (R2) must build a minimal env dict from scratch and the reuse must either (a) add an allowlist/replace-mode parameter to `build_env()`, or (b) have the wrapper bypass `build_env()` entirely. This is the single highest-leverage implementation finding for the V2 build.\n\n### Reuse-fit assessment\n- **Fits as-is:** stdin delivery, process-group kill, timeout→124, max_turns, stream-json, lifecycle hooks.\n- **Gap — no `cwd` parameter:** `start()` does **not** set `cwd` on the Popen call (lines 183–192); the child inherits the spawning process's cwd. Spec §7 says \"`cwd` = sandbox checkout\" but `ClaudeProcess` has **no `cwd` kwarg** [CODE-CONTRADICTED]. The Runner entrypoint must `os.chdir` into the checkout before instantiating, OR the primitive needs a new `cwd` parameter. Flag for TDD.\n- **Needs change:** env isolation (above); optional `cwd` parameter.\n\n**Key Takeaways**\n- `ClaudeProcess` at `process.py:72` is real and matches the spec's command/stdin claims exactly.\n- `build_env()` is additive-only (`os.environ.copy()`); the allowlist-env requirement (INV-001/SC-7/AC-7) is code-verified-necessary, not speculative.\n- No `cwd` parameter exists — spec §7's \"cwd = sandbox checkout\" needs either a new kwarg or a chdir in the Runner; flag for TDD.\n- Lifecycle hooks + `timeout_seconds` cleanly support the spec's audit events and `StuckRun` alert.\n\n---\n\n## V1.0 spec (`../20260610-234750-pr-review-auto-remediation/merged-requirements.md`)\n\nThe direct predecessor. Read in full.\n\n### What it does (the verified primitive)\n- **`class ClaudeProcess`** is defined at **line 72** [CODE-VERIFIED] — the spec's `cli/pipeline/process.py:72` citation is **accurate**.\n- Manages one `claude --print` subprocess with process-group signal handling (`os.setpgrp` → `os.killpg`) so the whole child tree dies on shutdown (`terminate()`, lines 278–323).\n- **`build_command()` (121–143)** emits exactly what the spec §7 claims: `claude --print --verbose --dangerously-skip-permissions --no-session-persistence --tools default --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` then `--model` (if set) then `extra_args`. Spec citation is **[CODE-VERIFIED]**.\n- **Prompt via stdin, not argv (162–217, 221–258):** `start()` writes the prompt to child stdin in 64 KiB chunks with EINTR-retry and BrokenPipe capture, closing stdin in `finally` to deliver EOF. This is the \"bypasses 128KB argv limit\" property the spec relies on for large prompt envelopes (§7). **[CODE-VERIFIED]** — the comment at lines 124–125 and 194–197 confirms the MAX_ARG_STRLEN rationale.\n- **Pre-spawn size guard:** `PromptTooLargeForArgv` raised when encoded prompt > `PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` (default 16 MiB, env-overridable via `SUPERCLAUDE_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`, lines 56–58, 169–173).\n- **Lifecycle hooks:** `on_spawn(pid)`, `on_signal(pid, sig)`, `on_exit(pid, rc)` — all optional. Useful for the spec's audit-log events (`claude_process_spawn`, §14) without subclassing.\n- **`timeout_seconds` default 6300** (~105 min); `wait()` returns `124` on timeout (bash-compatible). The spec's `StuckRun` alert (§14) keys on `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds` — that attribute is real and instance-accessible (line 109). **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n- **`tool_write_mode`** (101, 176–178, 325–345): when True, the LLM writes the output file via the Write tool and stdout is redirected to `.log`; `validate_tool_write_output()` confirms the file exists + is non-empty. Relevant if the Runner emits its diff via a tool-written artifact rather than stdout.\n- **`output_format`** default `\"stream-json\"` — matches spec §7's `stream-json` progress-capture requirement.\n\n### ⚠️ Critical gap the spec correctly flags — `build_env()` is NOT an allowlist [CODE-VERIFIED]\n`build_env()` (lines 145–160) does:\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full host environment\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # callers can only ADD/override, never restrict\nreturn env\n```\nThis is **additive-only**. A caller passing `env_vars={...}` cannot *remove* inherited secrets — `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `~/.aienv`-sourced vars, etc. all flow into the child via `os.environ.copy()`. The spec's §7 / INV-001 / SC-7 requirement (\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` … No `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\") is therefore a **real, code-grounded change**, not a hypothetical. AC-7 (Runner `/proc/<pid>/environ` contains no token) **cannot pass against the primitive as-is** — the Runner wrapper (R2) must build a minimal env dict from scratch and the reuse must either (a) add an allowlist/replace-mode parameter to `build_env()`, or (b) have the wrapper bypass `build_env()` entirely. This is the single highest-leverage implementation finding for the V2 build.\n\n### Reuse-fit assessment\n- **Fits as-is:** stdin delivery, process-group kill, timeout→124, max_turns, stream-json, lifecycle hooks.\n- **Gap — no `cwd` parameter:** `start()` does **not** set `cwd` on the Popen call (lines 183–192); the child inherits the spawning process's cwd. Spec §7 says \"`cwd` = sandbox checkout\" but `ClaudeProcess` has **no `cwd` kwarg** [CODE-CONTRADICTED]. The Runner entrypoint must `os.chdir` into the checkout before instantiating, OR the primitive needs a new `cwd` parameter. Flag for TDD.\n- **Needs change:** env isolation (above); optional `cwd` parameter.\n\n**Key Takeaways**\n- `ClaudeProcess` at `process.py:72` is real and matches the spec's command/stdin claims exactly.\n- `build_env()` is additive-only (`os.environ.copy()`); the allowlist-env requirement (INV-001/SC-7/AC-7) is code-verified-necessary, not speculative.\n- No `cwd` parameter exists — spec §7's \"cwd = sandbox checkout\" needs either a new kwarg or a chdir in the Runner; flag for TDD.\n- Lifecycle hooks + `timeout_seconds` cleanly support the spec's audit events and `StuckRun` alert.\n\n---\n\n## V1.0 spec (`../20260610-234750-pr-review-auto-remediation/merged-requirements.md`)\n\nThe direct predecessor. Every D*/R*/H* component in the spec's §2 inventory is genuinely **New**; only the four Reuse anchors (`ClaudeProcess`, swarm counter, severity rubric, auggie-review gh precedent) pre-exist. This is a from-scratch CLI group, not an edit of existing code.\n\n---\n\n## R2 — `ClaudeProcess` headless executor (`src/superclaude/cli/pipeline/process.py`)\n\nThe spec's single load-bearing reuse anchor. Read in full.\n\n### What it does (the verified primitive)\n- **`class ClaudeProcess`** is defined at **line 72** [CODE-VERIFIED] — the spec's `cli/pipeline/process.py:72` citation is **accurate**.\n- Manages one `claude --print` subprocess with process-group signal handling (`os.setpgrp` → `os.killpg`) so the whole child tree dies on shutdown (`terminate()`, lines 278–323).\n- **`build_command()` (121–143)** emits exactly what the spec §7 claims: `claude --print --verbose --dangerously-skip-permissions --no-session-persistence --tools default --max-turns N --output-format <fmt>` then `--model` (if set) then `extra_args`. Spec citation is **[CODE-VERIFIED]**.\n- **Prompt via stdin, not argv (162–217, 221–258):** `start()` writes the prompt to child stdin in 64 KiB chunks with EINTR-retry and BrokenPipe capture, closing stdin in `finally` to deliver EOF. This is the \"bypasses 128KB argv limit\" property the spec relies on for large prompt envelopes (§7). **[CODE-VERIFIED]** — the comment at lines 124–125 and 194–197 confirms the MAX_ARG_STRLEN rationale.\n- **Pre-spawn size guard:** `PromptTooLargeForArgv` raised when encoded prompt > `PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` (default 16 MiB, env-overridable via `SUPERCLAUDE_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`, lines 56–58, 169–173).\n- **Lifecycle hooks:** `on_spawn(pid)`, `on_signal(pid, sig)`, `on_exit(pid, rc)` — all optional. Useful for the spec's audit-log events (`claude_process_spawn`, §14) without subclassing.\n- **`timeout_seconds` default 6300** (~105 min); `wait()` returns `124` on timeout (bash-compatible). The spec's `StuckRun` alert (§14) keys on `ClaudeProcess.timeout_seconds` — that attribute is real and instance-accessible (line 109). **[CODE-VERIFIED]**\n- **`tool_write_mode`** (101, 176–178, 325–345): when True, the LLM writes the output file via the Write tool and stdout is redirected to `.log`; `validate_tool_write_output()` confirms the file exists + is non-empty. Relevant if the Runner emits its diff via a tool-written artifact rather than stdout.\n- **`output_format`** default `\"stream-json\"` — matches spec §7's `stream-json` progress-capture requirement.\n\n### ⚠️ Critical gap the spec correctly flags — `build_env()` is NOT an allowlist [CODE-VERIFIED]\n`build_env()` (lines 145–160) does:\n```python\nenv = os.environ.copy()          # full host environment\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDECODE\", None)\nenv.pop(\"CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT\", None)\nif env_vars:\n    env.update(env_vars)         # callers can only ADD/override, never restrict\nreturn env\n```\nThis is **additive-only**. A caller passing `env_vars={...}` cannot *remove* inherited secrets — `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `~/.aienv`-sourced vars, etc. all flow into the child via `os.environ.copy()`. The spec's §7 / INV-001 / SC-7 requirement (\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` … No `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\") is therefore a **real, code-grounded change**, not a hypothetical. AC-7 (Runner `/proc/<pid>/environ` contains no token) **cannot pass against the primitive as-is** — the Runner wrapper (R2) must build a minimal env dict from scratch and the reuse must either (a) add an allowlist/replace-mode parameter to `build_env()`, or (b) have the wrapper bypass `build_env()` entirely. This is the single highest-leverage implementation finding for the V2 build.\n\n### Reuse-fit assessment\n- **Fits as-is:** stdin delivery, process-group kill, timeout→124, max_turns, stream-json, lifecycle hooks.\n- **Gap — no `cwd` parameter:** `start()` does **not** set `cwd` on the Popen call (lines 183–192); the child inherits the spawning process's cwd. Spec §7 says \"`cwd` = sandbox checkout\" but `ClaudeProcess` has **no `cwd` kwarg** [CODE-CONTRADICTED]. The Runner entrypoint must `os.chdir` into the checkout before instantiating, OR the primitive needs a new `cwd` parameter. Spec citation is **[CODE-VERIFIED]**.\n- **Prompt via stdin, not argv (162–217, 221–258):** `start()` writes the prompt to child stdin in 64 KiB chunks with EINTR-retry and BrokenPipe capture, closing stdin in `finally` to deliver EOF. This is the \"bypasses 128KB argv limit\" property the spec relies on for large prompt envelopes (§7). **[CODE-VERIFIED]** — the comment at lines 124–125 and 194–197 confirms the MAX_ARG_STRLEN rationale.\n- **Pre-spawn size guard:** `PromptTooLargeForArgv` raised when encoded prompt > `PROMPT_MAX_BYTES` (default 16 MiB, env-overridable via `SUPERCLAUDE_PROMPT_MAX_BYTES`, lines 56–58, 169–173).\n- **Lifecycle hooks:** `on_spawn(pid)`, `on_signal(pid, sig)`, `on_exit(pid, rc)` — all optional. Useful for the spec's audit-log events (`claude_process_spawn`, §14) without subclassing.\n- **`timeout_seconds` default 6300** (~105 min); `wait()` returns `124` on timeout (bash-compatible). A caller passing `env_vars={...}` cannot *remove* inherited secrets — `GH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `~/.aienv`-sourced vars, etc. all flow into the child via `os.environ.copy()`. The spec's §7 / INV-001 / SC-7 requirement (\"`build_env()` MUST be wrapped with an explicit allowlist `env_vars` … No `GH_TOKEN`, no push credential in the Runner env\") is therefore a **real, code-grounded change**, not a hypothetical. AC-7 (Runner `/proc/<pid>/environ` contains no token) **cannot pass against the primitive as-is** — the Runner wrapper (R2) must build a minimal env dict from scratch and the reuse must either (a) add an allowlist/replace-mode parameter to `build_env()`, or (b) have the wrapper bypass `build_env()` entirely. This is the single highest-leverage implementation finding for the V2 build.\n\n### Reuse-fit assessment\n- **Fits as-is:** stdin delivery, process-group kill, timeout→124, max_turns, stream-json, lifecycle hooks.
